never authorize dispatch. Missing, changed or uninspectable identities refuse
the fault, including a checkpoint delayed twenty-two seconds. The existence
probe with signal number zero is read-only and confers no ownership.

Private fixture requests and keeper EOF still make living actors signal
themselves/their own groups. Those paths never turn disk records into signal
targets. Immediate birth checks on the remaining numeric fault paths do not
claim a kernel handle or eliminate every exit-to-syscall race. The
[FL round-4 report](../snapback4/reports/20261007-fl-dry-run-flakes/round-4/REPORT.md)
records the complete signal-site audit and intercepted negative controls.

**2026-10-07 — FL owned-command timeouts remain reapable.** Fault dispatch
from recorded PIDs still requires current command and birth. Direct-child
capture also retains the kernel's complete interpreter argv, including the
script path. Timeout/overflow cleanup uses the retained native ChildProcess
handle immediately and waits for close; it never converts that handle into an
unchecked numeric PID. It retains the original timeout/overflow. As corrected
in FL round 9, cleanup does not wait for initial capture or attempt another
identity inspection: either can outlive the command's watchdog. Fault
injection still requires fresh identity. Untracked objects and disk records
cannot use this cleanup path.

The Darwin inspector is compiled asynchronously before its identity API is
used: thirty seconds to finish, SIGTERM, one second of grace, then SIGKILL
and reap. The leaf probe has a ten-second timeout and uses its owned handle
for SIGKILL and reaping. FL round 9 makes that probe asynchronous too; pending
initial capture cancels its probe when the target child exits. Neither a
blocking probe nor a SIGTERM-only timeout may hold up sibling watchdogs.
Keeper EOF and fixture self-signals use a synchronous helper independent of
inspector compilation. [FL round 5](../snapback4/reports/20261007-fl-dry-run-flakes/round-5/REPORT.md)
records the hang regressions and their independent cleanup controls.

**2026-10-07 — FL compiler custody includes subprocesses.** Compilation now
runs beneath a live Node keeper in a private process group. The caller closes
its owned pipe to request TERM, then the keeper sends KILL to its own group
after the grace period, including when the compiler driver already exited.
Normal completion also releases the group. Caller death closes the pipe and
starts the same cleanup. The caller does not send to a cached PGID. Before
reporting completed cleanup it reaps the keeper and requires two complete
observations, 50 ms apart, with no live group member. Zombies are exited;
failed inspection is an explicit cleanup error. The bootstrap inspector uses
the system ps directly, without recursively compiling an identity inspector.
Processes deliberately leaving this group remain outside its custody.

Tracked direct-child dispatch now checks identity and then uses its retained
native handle. Recorded-PID dispatch still rechecks command and exact birth
immediately before the signal, but those userspace checks are not atomic with
the syscall: exit and PID reuse in that small interval can redirect a signal.
This is a remaining race, not an absolute guarantee against signalling an
unowned PID. Prefer group or native-handle custody for processes the test owns.
[FL round 6](../snapback4/reports/20261007-fl-dry-run-flakes/round-6/REPORT.md)
records the real GCC reproduction and subprocess regressions.

**2026-10-08 — FL nested correctness work is not a launch benchmark.** The
real runner CLI parity fixture, native Node baselines and export command
fixtures use the ordinary fifteen-minute file-stall floor as their command
work allowance. Multiple sequential invocations have an aggregate test guard
plus two minutes for cleanup. Their larger declared guards are reflected in
`largestGuard`, including its existing scheduling margin; the outer file
watchdog must not silently restore the old short limit. The export-background
cases share one work-plus-cleanup guard while keeping each existing state
observation and product-deadline assertion.

Nested CLI and ordinary native Node baselines await owned command completion
asynchronously. Timeout reports `TEST_FIXTURE_COMMAND_TIMEOUT` distinctly and
releases the command's private group; detached file keepers also receive EOF
when the runner exits. Native verdicts, counts, failure text, shim execution
and inspection/keeper-error refusals remain assertions. Deliberately exercised
watchdog and compiler-deadline clocks remain distinct from these correctness
work allowances. A real hung-runner control must still fail and drain its
descendants; delayed-startup controls must preserve all original verdicts.
Initial Linux child capture can observe an empty cmdline during an interpreter
exec. It may wait up to two minutes for complete bytes, anchored to the first
live birth and the spawning parent. Changed births, malformed nonempty commands
and other read failures refuse. **FL round 9:** this wait yields through
abortable ten-millisecond timers. Spawn returns the owned child immediately;
the command watchdog is armed before capture and cleanup never awaits it.
Child exit/error cancels pending capture, including a native probe. Signal
APIs are awaitable: callers must await initial capture and the fresh exact
command/birth checks before dispatch. Identity checks themselves do not retry
incomplete observations. No blocking wait or synchronous subprocess runs in
the shared runner, signal or command helpers. Recorded-PID inspection is still
not atomic with the syscall; owned handles/groups remain preferred. The
[round-9 report](../snapback4/reports/20261007-fl-dry-run-flakes/round-9/REPORT.md)
records heartbeat, timeout/reaping and anchor mutation controls.
Load verification must also keep its executable inode stable: rebuilding a
CLI whose live workers re-exec `current_exe()` invalidates that concurrent run.
See [FL round 8](../snapback4/reports/20261007-fl-dry-run-flakes/round-8/REPORT.md).

## 9. Architecture and code review, 2026-09-12

The author's request for a comprehensive review adds a findings record to
this root before opening focused research children. The review covers the
engine, compiler, HTTP/CLI host, both client tiers and stores, React arms,
reference apps, simulator, fixtures, witnesses and release tooling at
Snapback 4 tree `cf0a13dca32b3e1721d1d947a3f82c262a0807f0`
(repository commit `d6c803878a5432cce5f045777786a049972d3d70`, version
0.0.16). It does not change the bakeoff decisions or accept new designs.

The prescribed suite passes, but additional probes reproduce
schema-admission and projection failures, lost or regressed replica state,
incorrect predictions, stale observations, compiler semantic errors and
request-worker starvation. Passing the existing suite does not establish
the missing properties. The author's updated scope reserves security holes
for another model; this record covers architecture, correctness,
performance and ergonomics and makes no security-review claim.

Four questions need design work beyond an isolated patch:

- [LLP 3000.000](./3000.000-atomic-schema-and-release-activation.research.md):
  the atomic unit and owner of schema/release activation.
- [LLP 3000.001](./3000.001-durable-replica-and-write-lifecycle.research.md):
  confirmed replica data, speculative writes and durable outcomes.
- [LLP 3000.002](./3000.002-end-to-end-resource-bounds.research.md):
  physical resource bounds across requests and replication.
- [LLP 3000.003](./3000.003-one-semantics-and-credible-verification.research.md):
  one semantic contract across Rust, TypeScript and verification hosts.

The [detailed review and 37-ticket index](./reviews/3000-architecture-code-review-20260912.md)
record evidence, alternatives and acceptance examples. All four children are
Draft Research. They remain review findings and proposals; the core owner
retains implementation custody under §1 decision 4.

The author subsequently requested implementation of the 37 concrete tickets.
All 37 are now closed; the [correction qualification record](./reviews/3000-review-corrections-20260913.md)
contains the executed checks, independent reviews and remaining research scope.
Their resolution records and the children's `Implementation follow-up`
sections describe the delivered boundaries: transactional semantic activation,
confirmed-base replay and atomic snapshot ownership, bounded admission/output,
and compiler/client parity corrections. Integration preserves the concurrently
landed native Expo module and ordered-merge performance work. The four children
remain Draft Research for their explicitly outstanding choices; the ticket
fixes do not claim that every architecture question has been settled.

The separate [security review](./reviews/3000-security-review-20260913.md)
opened twelve findings across the HTTP, identity, storage, synchronization and
asset boundaries. Its
[remediation record](./reviews/3000-security-remediation-20260913.md) closes
those findings and three overlapping comprehensive-review findings. The four
security decisions in §7 state the lasting contract; individual tickets retain
the counterexamples, acceptance evidence and limits.

## How to orient

Read this document, then LLP 2000.000 for the argument and its evidence,
then `snapback4/AGENTS.md` once it exists, then the journal. For the
sibling's position read LLP 2000; for the incumbent, LLP 1000; for the
history both inherit, LLP 0329 and its family.

## Non-claims

- This document accepts nothing in LLP 2000.000 on the author's behalf; it
  is the brief, and only running code binds.
- It changes no Snapback 2 or Snapback 3 status, scope, ceiling or gate, and
  edits neither rules file.
- It decides no promotion; the comparison and its ruling are the author's.
- It claims no result; every number above is a threshold or a plan.

## 2026-09-23 — Clique 0.3 follow-up implementation commission

Charlie commissioned Astra max or xhigh to implement the accepted
[modeling plan](./3000.011.000.001-modeling-after-clique-030.plan.md) and
[scale plan](./3000.011.001.001-scale-after-clique-030.plan.md). Their earlier
sanity review found no material concerns. Implementation preserves each plan’s
measurement-first sequence and stopping conditions. Historical research links
3000.000 and 3000.001 leave the current overlay to make room; the documents
remain. Package publication and mutation of the frozen benchmark are outside
this commission.

## 2026-09-27 — Global search beyond one read

Lane GS reproduced the global-search boundary and stopped at a design handoff.
The supervisor narrowed 0.4.6 to compact, bounded one-read membership search;
[LLP 3000.024](./3000.024-global-search-beyond-one-read.rfc.md) records the
Decision, implementation, measurements and explicit grouped-search refusal
beyond its envelope. The retained-session and stateless multi-pass contracts
remain future RFC work. Budgets, disclosure rules and the no-query-cache
exclusion are unchanged; round 1's report records qualification failures.

Marker and recovery certificate COMMIT messages explicitly set synchronous
commit and locally disable statement/lock timeouts. These settings share the
existing message, preserve the marker's exact command-count certificate, and
leave the session's five-second settings intact after the transaction.

Deterministic witnesses supersede three consecutive recovery attempts from a
reader thread, observe restored write admission after the follow-up certificate,
and exercise queued replies plus lease-loss retry cessation. Real marker lock
and recovery COMMIT waits exceed five seconds. The before tree fails the
concurrent-break, marker-lock and recovery-COMMIT witnesses; the additional
queue/settings witnesses and final requested checks are recorded in
[the G7-E report](reports/20261025-g7e-postgres/REPORT.md).


## 2026-10-26 — G7-E fix round 2: queue hint boundary

The `f9a870b262` hint audit found that queue errors bypass the mutation result
continuation entirely, including its failure eviction. The caller now evicts
the carried hint on that outer error too. The new HTTP witness warms a hint,
loses queue ownership, and checks that the refusal carries no store metadata
and leaves no hint. It fails before with the old hint retained and passes after.
The existing held-marker success/unknown witnesses still gate hint publication
on certified release. A late eviction of a newer certified observation only
adds cold discovery; hints never authorize, and every execution checks fresh
store, generation, principal, kind and Full scope.

The core recovery/timeout change is `5eae13de38`. The six new regressions all
have failing-before and passing-after evidence. Full core is 1,309/0 (523
ignored), binary 623/0 (75 ignored), E core/HTTP 39/0 and 5/0, G7.2 40/0,
lever core/HTTP 2/0 and 87/0, RT0 core/HTTP 6/0 and 10/0, and crash/kill 11/0
with zero acknowledged loss. Final Postgres, reference and process-custody
results are recorded in
[the G7-E report](reports/20261025-g7e-postgres/REPORT.md).

Final checks: Postgres Rust targets **816/1**, only inherited `E_INPUT: 6`;
the evaluator's inherited stale lockfile blocks its known `40001` stage.
Public S/E serving, full binary build and SQLite-only feature check pass.
Ref-check: 569 LLPs, 12,033 references, zero errors/unchecked.
All 7 private cluster directories confirm stopped; the combined
12,623-pair audit, including zombies and both public PID ledgers,
has no matches. Minimum monitored free disk: 59.35 GiB.
**PIDs left: none.** No throughput campaign, publication or push.

### 2026-10-26 — DX12: test the real npm serve script

`serve-check --then` and `withServeScript` extend the DX10 keeper/process-group
and pre-forced-cleanup lock barrier. Readiness stays private; commands receive
it through environment and callbacks in memory. Command status, startup/proof
deadlines and unsuccessful npm shutdown are separate observations. Password
probe starters accept --then. Development real-user setup now shares the
production account plan; rendered text scrolls unique reachable candidates,
including propagated Expo body overflow, while ordinary clips still exclude
text. beginForm constrains named arguments rather than silently losing a claim
when the handler adds an id. LLP 3000.011.003.000.000 §17 records the contracts.
Linux packed/browser/process regressions pass; native Mac execution is skipped.

### 2026-10-26 — DX12: short release output

Deploy uses DX11's render_mode: gates/refutations remain actionable; advisory
decisions are counted and expanded with --review. Activation leads with verdict
then REL's live identity and keeps validation previews behind --verbose.
Releases is compact by default and retains structured evidence with --json.
Fifty identical copied wave-29 schemas deploy successfully in both controls:
median complete stdout+stderr is 33 lines with published 0.4.42 and 5 with DX12.
All eight Muse serve/client-call pairs finish through the kit, with every lock
released: 141.178 s total, median 2.410 s. The two slow call scripts themselves
take about a minute; no new builder-time savings are claimed. Existing release
and security semantics are unchanged (REL2/SEC10 own those). LLP 3000.036.004
§19.1 records presentation; detailed evidence is in reports/20261026-dx12.

// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#17-wave-29-serve-proofs-and-proof-residue-dx12 — callbacks and commands share the npm serve group and cleanup barrier.
import { spawn, execFile } from "node:child_process";
import { promisify } from "node:util";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { resolve } from "node:path";
import { setTimeout as sleep } from "node:timers/promises";
import { bounded, deadline, register } from "./proof-process.ts";
import { cli } from "./proof.ts";
export interface ServeScriptReady {
  backendUrl?: string; frontendUrl?: string; [key: string]: unknown;
}
export interface ServeCheckOptions {
  cwd?: string;
  env?: NodeJS.ProcessEnv;
  script?: string;
  /** Deployment whose kernel lock must be released after group SIGTERM. */
  deployment: string;
  /** Startup deadline. Default 30 seconds. */
  timeoutMs?: number;
  /** Time to remain up after readiness. Default 30 seconds. */
  upMs?: number;
  stopTimeoutMs?: number;
  /** Run after readiness, inside the owned group (shell command) or callback scope. */
  then?: string | ((ready: ServeScriptReady, signal: AbortSignal) => unknown | Promise<unknown>);
  /** Command/callback deadline. Default 120 seconds. */
  thenTimeoutMs?: number;
}
export interface ServeCheckReport {
  ok: boolean;
  script: string;
  failures: Array<{code:string; message:string}>;
  ready: boolean;
  upMs: number;
  lockReleased: boolean;
  commandCode?: number;
}
/** Executes npm run --silent SCRIPT in a private Unix process group.
 * Credentials in script stdout are never returned or written to logs.
 * @throws invalid options, unsupported platform, or inability to inspect/reap the owned group. */
export async function serveCheck(options: ServeCheckOptions): Promise<ServeCheckReport> {
  if (process.platform === "win32") throw Error("E_SERVE_CHECK_PLATFORM: process groups require Unix");
  const script = options.script ?? "prod:serve";
  if (!/^[A-Za-z0-9_:.-]+$/.test(script) || !options.deployment) throw Error("E_PROOF_USAGE: serveCheck requires a script name and deployment");
  if (options.then !== undefined && typeof options.then !== "function" && (typeof options.then !== "string" || !options.then.trim())) throw Error("E_PROOF_USAGE: then needs a command or callback");
  const thenTimeout = deadline(options.thenTimeoutMs, 120_000);
  const startup = deadline(options.timeoutMs, 30_000), up = deadline(options.upMs, 30_000), stop = deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000);
  const cwd = resolve(options.cwd ?? "."), failures: ServeCheckReport["failures"] = [];
  const add = (code:string, message:string) => { if (!failures.some(f => f.code === code)) failures.push({code,message}); };
  const child = spawn(process.execPath, [fileURLToPath(new URL("./proof-serve-driver.js", import.meta.url)), script], {
    cwd, env:{...process.env, ...options.env}, detached:true, stdio:["pipe", "pipe", "pipe", "ipc"],
  });
  register(child); child.stdin!.on("error", () => {});
  let serveExitCode: number | null | undefined, serveExitSignal: string | null | undefined;
  let keeper = 0, exited = false, ready = false, buffer = "", lineCount = 0, bytes = 0;
  let spawnError: Error | undefined, cleaning = false;
  const lostDriver = () => { if (!cleaning) add("E_SERVE_DRIVER_EXIT", "serve-check driver or keeper exited before inspection completed"); };
  child.once("exit", lostDriver); child.once("disconnect", lostDriver);
  child.on("error", error => { spawnError = error; });
  let commandCode: number | undefined, readyValue: ServeScriptReady = {};
  child.on("message", message => {
    const command = message as {type:string;code?:number;stream?:string;data?:string};
    if (command.type === "then-result") commandCode = command.code ?? 1;
    if (command.type === "then-output" && typeof command.data === "string") (command.stream === "stderr" ? process.stderr : process.stdout).write(command.data);
    const event = message as {type:string;keeper?:number;code?:number|null;signal?:string|null}; if (event.type === "started") keeper = event.keeper!; if (event.type === "exited") { exited = true; serveExitCode = event.code; serveExitSignal = event.signal; } });
  const closed = new Promise<void>(resolve => child.once("close", () => resolve()));
  child.stdout!.setEncoding("utf8"); child.stdout!.on("data", chunk => {
    bytes += Buffer.byteLength(chunk);
    if (bytes > 1024 * 1024) { add("E_SERVE_EXTRA_OUTPUT", "stdout exceeds 1 MiB; output withheld"); buffer = ""; return; }
    buffer += chunk;
    for (;;) {
      const at = buffer.indexOf("\n"); if (at < 0) break;
      const line = buffer.slice(0, at); buffer = buffer.slice(at + 1); lineCount++;
      try {
        const value = JSON.parse(line);
        if (!ready && lineCount === 1 && value && typeof value === "object" && !Array.isArray(value)) { ready = true; readyValue = value; }
        else add("E_SERVE_EXTRA_OUTPUT", "stdout must contain exactly one JSON object line");
      } catch { add("E_SERVE_EXTRA_OUTPUT", "non-JSON stdout (including npm banners/blank lines); use npm run --silent and keep diagnostics on stderr"); }
      if (lineCount > 1) add("E_SERVE_EXTRA_OUTPUT", "stdout must contain exactly one JSON object line");
    }
  });
  child.stderr!.resume(); // May contain credentials from application diagnostics; never persist or echo it.
  const members = async () => {
    const {stdout} = await promisify(execFile)("ps", ["-axo", "pid=,pgid=,stat="], {timeout:5000, maxBuffer:16 * 1024 * 1024});
    return stdout.trim().split("\n").filter(Boolean).map(line => {
      const match = /^\s*(\d+)\s+(\d+)\s+(\S+)\s*$/.exec(line);
      if (!match) throw Error("E_SERVE_INSPECTION: unrecognized ps row");
      return {pid:Number(match[1]), group:Number(match[2]), state:match[3]!};
    }).filter(row => row.group === child.pid && row.pid !== child.pid && row.pid !== keeper && !row.state.startsWith("Z"));
  };
  let lockReleased = false, observedUp = 0, callbackError: unknown;

    const end = performance.now() + startup;
    while ((!ready || !keeper) && !exited && !spawnError && child.exitCode === null && child.signalCode === null && performance.now() < end) await sleep(20);
    if (!keeper) add("E_SERVE_START", "process-group keeper handshake was not received");
    if (spawnError) add("E_SERVE_START", "npm script could not start");
    if (!ready) add("E_SERVE_NO_JSON", `npm run --silent ${script} ${exited ? "exited without" : `did not emit within ${startup} ms`} a first JSON object line; inspect the serve script startup`);
    if (ready && !exited && options.then !== undefined && failures.length === 0) {
      const began = performance.now();
      const controller = new AbortController();
      try {
        if (typeof options.then === "string") {
          child.send({type:"then", command:options.then, ready:readyValue});
          while (commandCode === undefined && !exited && child.exitCode === null && child.signalCode === null && performance.now() - began < thenTimeout) await sleep(20);
          if (commandCode === undefined && !exited && child.exitCode === null && child.signalCode === null) add("E_SERVE_THEN_TIMEOUT", `command did not exit within ${thenTimeout} ms; owned command and serve group will be stopped`);
        } else {
          await bounded(Promise.resolve().then(() => (options.then as Function)(readyValue, controller.signal)), thenTimeout, "E_SERVE_THEN_TIMEOUT: callback");
        }
      } catch (error) { callbackError = error; add(error instanceof Error && error.message.startsWith("E_SERVE_THEN_TIMEOUT") ? "E_SERVE_THEN_TIMEOUT" : "E_SERVE_THEN_FAILED", "proof callback failed; cleanup still checks group shutdown and lock release"); } finally { controller.abort(); observedUp = Math.round(performance.now() - began); }
    } else if (ready && !exited && options.then === undefined) {
      const began = performance.now();
      while (!exited && child.exitCode === null && child.signalCode === null && performance.now() - began < up) await sleep(Math.min(25, Math.max(1, up - (performance.now() - began))));
      observedUp = Math.round(performance.now() - began);
    }
    if (exited) add("E_SERVE_EARLY_EXIT", "npm serve script exited before the running proof completed");
    if (keeper && child.exitCode === null && child.signalCode === null) {
      process.kill(-child.pid!, "SIGTERM");
      const end = performance.now() + stop;
      let living = await members();
      while (living.length && performance.now() < end) { await sleep(25); living = await members(); }
      if (living.length) add("E_SERVE_CHILD_RUNNING", "child left running after group SIGTERM");
    }
    if ((serveExitCode !== undefined && serveExitCode !== null && serveExitCode !== 0) || (serveExitSignal && serveExitSignal !== "SIGTERM")) add("E_SERVE_STOP_FAILED", `npm serve script shutdown failed (${serveExitCode ?? serveExitSignal}); inspect shutdown handlers`);
    if (buffer.length) add("E_SERVE_EXTRA_OUTPUT", "stdout contains an unterminated line");
    // Observe lock BEFORE the keeper kills leftovers: forced cleanup cannot earn a pass.
    const lock = await cli(["serve", resolve(cwd, options.deployment), "--wait-stopped"], {cwd, ...(options.env ? {env:options.env} : {}), timeoutMs:7000});
    lockReleased = lock.code === 0;
    if (!lockReleased) add("E_SERVE_LOCK_HELD", "deployment lock held after group SIGTERM (or lock barrier refused)");
  } finally {
    cleaning = true;
    child.stdin!.destroy(); // keeper EOF kills only its pinned group, including itself.
    await bounded(closed, 7000, "serve-check group reap");
    child.stdout!.destroy(); child.stderr!.destroy();
  }
  // Pipe bytes may be delivered while the lock barrier is running or during final drain.
  if (buffer.length) add("E_SERVE_EXTRA_OUTPUT", "stdout contains an unterminated line");
  if ((serveExitCode !== undefined && serveExitCode !== null && serveExitCode !== 0) || (serveExitSignal && serveExitSignal !== "SIGTERM")) add("E_SERVE_STOP_FAILED", `npm serve script shutdown failed (${serveExitCode ?? serveExitSignal}); inspect shutdown handlers`);
  const report = {ok:failures.length === 0 && (commandCode === undefined || commandCode === 0), script, failures, ready, upMs:observedUp, lockReleased, ...(commandCode === undefined ? {} : {commandCode})};
  if (callbackError !== undefined) throw Object.assign(callbackError instanceof Error ? callbackError : Error("serve callback failed"), {report});
  return report;
}

/** Run a proof against the app's npm serve script; always stop its group and await lock release.
 * Readiness can include credentials: keep it in memory and never log it.
 * Callback work must honor signal or bound its own external resources.
 * @throws callback failure, deadline, or E_SERVE_CHECK with a credential-free report. */
export async function withServeScript<T>(script: string, fn: (ready: ServeScriptReady, signal: AbortSignal) => T | Promise<T>, options: Omit<ServeCheckOptions, "script" | "then" | "upMs">): Promise<T> {
  let value!: T;
  const report = await serveCheck({...options, script, then: async (ready, signal) => { value = await fn(ready, signal); }});
  if (!report.ok) throw Object.assign(Error(`E_SERVE_CHECK: ${report.failures.map(f => `${f.code}: ${f.message}`).join("; ")}`), {report});
  return value;
}

## 16. Wave-27 process checks (DX10)

Implementer: DX10, 2026-10-24, wave-27 §10b #4/#6. `serveCheck` and the packaged
`serve-check --deployment DIR --script NAME` execute `npm run --silent NAME` in
a private Unix group. One JSON object line is required; stdout stays in memory
and never appears in its report/logs because it can contain credentials. Keep
alive for 30 seconds after readiness (configurable), then group SIGTERM. Report
missing JSON, extra output, early exit, remaining live non-zombie children and
unreleased store ownership separately. Inspect group and lock before forced
cleanup, so cleanup cannot erase the failure. The keeper ignores TERM, pins the
group during inspection, and kills only its own group on driver EOF. Detached
children that deliberately leave the group remain outside supported custody.
Windows refuses; Mac execution is deferred to integration. Inspection failure, driver exit or IPC loss before authorized cleanup
rejects, never passes. Stage recipes use the ordinary live-owner control path
or exclusive cold move, and await each operation and owned-host stop.

Production account setup reuses a named local roster under the deployment,
with private fsynced atomic snapshots (file, containing directory and new directory link before effects) and an exclusive per-roster setup lock. The
startServe handle binds its deployment directory; standalone hosts must name
rosterDir. A stable role/count, mutation and service plan is required. Persist
random credentials before signup; on resumption first try login. A known
principal that cannot log in refuses instead of creating a replacement. Persist
principal, arguments and mutation ID before provisioning; replay an unfinished
mutation with that same ID under ordinary server deduplication. Completed
entries only log in, never provision again. Changed wire arguments/plans refuse; argument object key order is canonicalized recursively.
No authority bypass, grant repair or rollback promise is added. Removing or
renaming a roster explicitly requests new identities; preserve it with its
store. A stale exclusive lock requires operator inspection, never automatic
stealing. Staging seedAccounts retains its fresh-batch behavior.

Validation uses real production restarts and interrupted replies, process driver
faults, literal guide stage scripts, and the packed package. Credentials are
never included in committed reports. Gate semantic gaps are handed to SEC10;
DX10's write-advice changes only prioritize the explicit update-only answer.

## 17. Wave-29 serve proofs and proof residue (DX12)

DX12 extends §16's same npm script/process-group/first-JSON-line custody with
`serve-check --then '<cmd>'` and `withServeScript(script, fn, options)`. The
script's readiness JSON stays in memory. A command receives SNAPBACK4_SERVE_JSON,
SNAPBACK_URL/BACKEND_URL and FRONTEND_URL; a callback receives the object and an
AbortSignal. Neither report nor runner logs persist readiness. The shell command
runs in the same keeper-owned group as the serve, so descendants cannot survive
normal or deadline cleanup without a reported failure. The command's nonzero
exit status is preserved. A successful command cannot erase a serve startup,
lifetime, observed npm shutdown or lock-release failure. Startup and proof deadlines
are independent; a hung command names its deadline. Cleanup observes remaining
children and the store lock before forced group reaping, as §16 requires.
Callback failure propagates after cleanup; callback-owned resources must honor
the signal or supply their own bounds. Arbitrary JavaScript promises cannot be
forcibly cancelled. Escaping process groups remain outside custody.

All recipes for testing an existing npm serve point to this runner, including
probe:check, staging and day-two business assertions. Direct startServe remains
a kit primitive for constructing an app's serve script or a disposable kit-owned
multi-host fixture; builders need no custom npm-script driver.

`renderedText` discovers reachable text through legal scroll ranges and scrolls
a unique match into view before observation. Root overflow propagated to the
viewport does not clip Expo web text to the non-scrolling body box. Ambiguity,
hidden/editable text and non-scrollable clips retain their refusals. This
supersedes §12's observation-without-scrolling behavior.

`devAccounts(startDevHost, plan)` (also a DevHost method) uses the same role/count,
provisioning mutation, service seat, custom args and role-keyed credential shape
as production. It requires development password identity, signs up actual users
and invokes ordinary authorized provisioning. It neither seeds tables nor
relaxes production/staging refusal checks. Development batches are fresh;
production roster persistence remains §16's contract and REL2's lock repair.

`beginForm`'s argument object constrains its named keys: handler-owned extra
arguments are accepted, nested named values still compare canonical JSON
exactly, and null/undefined leaves arguments unconstrained. This supersedes
§10's whole-object equality and fixes partial claims dropping the receipt.
Trusted click/synchronous submit, handler claims, durable-op selection and
actual write receipts remain required. Named value mismatches still settle an
explicit E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED receipt without preventing the application's write.

Verification uses packed consumers for changed recipes and starters, process
fault/deadline/status/lock tests, development real-user reads, and browser tests
of Expo root scrolling, hidden/ambiguous text and subset/value mismatch claims.
Wave-29 replay inputs are copied read-only from the evidence into temporary
workspaces, measurements exclude credentials, and copies are deleted afterwards.
Darwin/native execution is skipped in this Linux workstream.

DX12 shutdown-observation boundary: this deliberately uses the harness's group
SIGTERM, including npm and its shell. Nonzero npm exits that the driver receives
are failures. A SIGTERM exit plus an empty group and released lock proves those
process/ownership observations; it does not prove that every application's
shutdown hook completed successfully. npm or an intermediate shell may terminate
before collecting a descendant's exit status. A synthetic handler installed in
npm tests the observable-exit branch only; it is not shipped or required. No
claim about every descendant's exit code is made. Applications needing external
cleanup guarantees must assert those effects in their proof command/callback.
### 19.1 Deploy, rollback and release inspection (DX12)

Apply §19's short default to deploy, rollback and releases. Completed activation
prints verdict then REL's `live:` identity. A refused command prints the refusal
verdict and actionable gates/refutations; derived acknowledged candidates remain
review evidence. Default deploy uses the same `render_mode` decision filter as
check. Advisory decisions move behind --review with a one-line count and next
command. --verbose also retains them and expands previews; successful activation
does not reprint its validation preview. Explicit --check keeps visibility and
hazards with next steps; verbose expands grouped notes and storage detail.
`releases DIR` prints compact identity/evidence counts; --json preserves its
structured contract and --review/--verbose retains expanded inspection. JSON
security reports keep all decisions and evidence. Gate registry, binding,
acknowledgment and release semantics are unchanged; SEC and REL own those.

Verification compares wave-29 copied schemas' deploy transcripts before/after,
asserts verdict/live ordering, advisory opt-in and structured evidence, and
retains a blocking-gate regression with its repair and acknowledgment commands.

## 18. Wave-26 security decisions (DX9)

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-24. Revision 3: Muse and fresh Codex READY; implementation is in the DX9 commits. Qualification and limitations: WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md at the worktree root.
This supersedes §17.3's prohibition on family disposition only for the
advisory audience/restatement family. Gates remain individual specification
decisions, including every new SEC10 gate; one gate registry decides ranking.
The default check summary names the number of pending decisions and the next
command, even when callers read only ^check:. Each review-tier item appears
once as a question with one-line alternatives: repair enforcement, record an
intentional specification decision, or add independent intent/test evidence.
Performance/design information remains separate. JSON retains every original
diagnostic, site, ID, source binding and acknowledgment. Gate explanation and
disposition command share one decision entry, never a second note listing.

check --ack-security audience:<family-id> --reason '<spec rationale>' records
one rationale for the exact displayed set of current non-gated audience notes.
The eligible codes are exactly N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE, N_ROLE_WIDE_READ,
N_ROLE_WIDE_WRITE and N_BROAD_READ, excluding any code in GATED_NOTE_CODES.
The family ID hashes analyzer revision sec9-3, their sorted individual IDs and
semantic source bindings;
each member is still written as an individual existing version-1 record. A
new member, changed dependency, unknown/stale family, blank reason, compilation
failure or reachable refutation cannot be cleared. No gate, derived contract,
non-audience heuristic, or authored contradiction enters this family. Existing
writer lock and source race checks bind the complete set. All member entries
are added in memory and published in one temp+rename of security-reviews.json;
there is no partial-family write. A crash before rename leaves the prior file.
The individual record analyzer revision also becomes sec9-3.
Output names the member subjects and count so the command is reviewable.

The static rule analyzer's Verdict.direct_rule=Proved alongside
Verdict.status=Unproved is rendered as
"enforced by rule; additional paths not proved", with exact blockers in verbose
and JSON (Verdict.reasons, Verdict.obligations and their paths/status/repair).
Whole-invariant Proved/Refuted/Unproved semantics never change, and
no finite campaign promotes a proof. A check line counts asset-bearing columns (ColumnType.contains_asset),
Table.column_reads entries, and N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION diagnostics. Asset owner
custody is the runtime asset ledger/placement contract, not a linter theorem.
Role diagnostics use the existing lexical authority walker (helper depth 32)
and existing bounded privilege analyzer; the line says "detected (bounded
analysis)" and does not characterize unreachable/unsupported paths as safe. Never print an unconditional zero-role-hole claim.

The old N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE detector is folded into the single
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";

import type { Locator, FrameLocator, Selectors } from "playwright-core";

export const renderedSelector = `sb4rendered${randomUUID().replaceAll("-", "")}`;
const registrations = new WeakMap<Selectors, Promise<void>>();

    selectorReady = Promise.all([selectorReady, registration]).then(() => {});
    void selectorReady.catch(() => {}); // Observations await and surface this rejection.

          scaleY = html.offsetHeight ? box.height / html.offsetHeight : 1;

        const scrollY = scrollCoupled && /^(auto|scroll)$/.test(style.overflowY) && html.clientHeight > 0;

    if (!scrollCoupled) clips.push({ box: new DOMRect(0, 0, document.documentElement.clientWidth, document.documentElement.clientHeight), x: true, y: true, dx: [0, 0], dy: [0, 0] });

  function queryAll(root: Document | Element | ShadowRoot, selector: string): Element[] {
    const options = JSON.parse(selector) as { source: string; flags: string; regex: boolean; exact: boolean };
    const regex = options.regex ? new RegExp(options.source, options.flags) : null;
    const needle = options.source.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
    const found: Element[] = [];
    const matches = (text: string) => {
      if (!text) return false;
      if (regex) { regex.lastIndex = 0; return regex.test(text); }
      return options.exact ? text === needle : text.toLowerCase().includes(needle.toLowerCase());
    };
    const walk = (node: Node, ancestorSuppressed = false): { text: string; matched: boolean } => {
      if (node.nodeType === Node.TEXT_NODE) return { text: ancestorSuppressed ? "" : textInView(node), matched: false };
      if (node.nodeType !== Node.ELEMENT_NODE && node.nodeType !== Node.DOCUMENT_NODE && node.nodeType !== Node.DOCUMENT_FRAGMENT_NODE) return { text: "", matched: false };
      const element = node.nodeType === Node.ELEMENT_NODE ? node as HTMLElement : null;
      if (element && (/^(INPUT|TEXTAREA|SCRIPT|STYLE|NOSCRIPT|TEMPLATE)$/.test(element.tagName) || element.isContentEditable)) return { text: "", matched: false };
      const style = element ? getComputedStyle(element) : null;
      if (style?.display === "none" || style?.opacity === "0") return { text: "", matched: false };
      const suppressed = style ? style.visibility === "hidden" || style.visibility === "collapse" : ancestorSuppressed;
      const nodes = element?.shadowRoot ? element.shadowRoot.childNodes
        : element?.tagName === "SLOT" ? ((element as HTMLSlotElement).assignedNodes({flatten:true}).length ? (element as HTMLSlotElement).assignedNodes({flatten:true}) : element.childNodes) : node.childNodes;
      let text = "", descendantMatch = false;
      for (const child of nodes) {
        const result = walk(child, suppressed);
        // Block boundaries are whitespace, never concatenated words from
        // unrelated paragraph siblings. Inline RN spans retain their text.
        const block = child.nodeType === Node.ELEMENT_NODE && /^(block|flex|grid|table|list-item)/.test(getComputedStyle(child as Element).display);
        text += block ? ` ${result.text} ` : result.text;
        descendantMatch ||= result.matched;
      }
      const normalized = text.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
      const box = element?.getBoundingClientRect();
      const visible = element && !suppressed && box && box.width > 0 && box.height > 0;
      const ownMatch = !!visible && !/^(HTML|BODY)$/.test(element.tagName) && matches(normalized);
      if (ownMatch && !descendantMatch) found.push(element);
      return { text, matched: ownMatch || descendantMatch };
    };
    observedText = walk(root).text.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
    return found;
  }
  return { queryAll, query: (root: Document | Element | ShadowRoot, selector: string) => queryAll(root, selector)[0] ?? null,
    text: (root: Element) => { queryAll(root, '{"source":"","flags":"","regex":false,"exact":true}'); return observedText; } };
}

export function visibleText(locator: Locator, timeout: number): Promise<string> {
  // Build the function in Node, then let Playwright serialize it. Passing an
  // arrow as a string would evaluate to the function rather than invoke it.
  const reader = new Function("element", `return (${renderedTextEngine.toString()})().text(element);`) as (element: Element) => string;
  return locator.evaluate(reader, undefined, { timeout });
}

// Optional peer: CLI/process helpers still import and run when it is absent.
// Keep this graph synchronously require-able. Registration starts during import;
// withBrowser awaits that same promise before creating its context. Caller-owned
// browsers finish asynchronous launch before running a selector.
try { void registerRenderedSelector(createRequire(import.meta.url)("playwright-core").selectors).catch(() => {}); }
catch (error) { if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND" && (error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "MODULE_NOT_FOUND") throw error; }

// Locator construction stays synchronous for CommonJS callers. Actions wait
// for optional-peer selector registration, including when the page predates
// the proof import. Preserve synchronous Locator/FrameLocator chaining.
export const scopedLocators = new WeakSet<object>();
const locatorWrappers = new WeakMap<object, object>();
const locatorChains = new Set(["locator", "filter", "describe", "first", "last", "nth", "and", "or", "frameLocator", "contentFrame", "owner"]);
export function registeredLocator<T extends Locator | FrameLocator>(locator: T): T {
  const known = locatorWrappers.get(locator);
  if (known) return known as T;
  const wrapped = new Proxy(locator, {
    get(target, key) {
      const value = Reflect.get(target, key, target);
      if (typeof value !== "function" || key === "constructor") return value;
      if (typeof key !== "string" || key === "page" || key === "toString" || key === "_inspect" || key === "_equals") return value.bind(target);
      if (locatorChains.has(key) || key.startsWith("getBy")) return (...args: unknown[]) => {
        const next = registeredLocator(Reflect.apply(value, target, args));
        if (scopedLocators.has(wrapped) || scopedLocators.has(target)) scopedLocators.add(next);
        return next;
      };
      return (...args: unknown[]) => selectorReady.then(async () => {
        if (["count", "isVisible", "innerText", "textContent", "evaluate"].includes(key) && "count" in target) {
          const candidate = target as Locator;
          if (await candidate.count() === 1) await candidate.scrollIntoViewIfNeeded({timeout:1000});
        }
        return Reflect.apply(value, target, args);
      });
    },
  });
  locatorWrappers.set(locator, wrapped); locatorWrappers.set(wrapped, wrapped);
  return wrapped;
}

import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
process.stdin.resume();
process.on("SIGTERM", () => {}); process.on("SIGINT", () => {});
if (process.argv[2] === "--keeper") {
  process.stdin.once("end", () => process.kill(0, "SIGKILL"));
  process.stdin.once("error", () => process.kill(0, "SIGKILL"));
  process.send?.("ready");
} else {
  const guard = spawn(process.execPath, [fileURLToPath(import.meta.url), "--keeper"], {stdio:["pipe", "ignore", "inherit", "ipc"]});
  guard.stdin!.on("error", () => {});
  process.stdin.once("end", () => guard.stdin!.destroy());
  process.stdin.once("error", () => guard.stdin!.destroy());
  await new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => { guard.once("message", () => resolve()); guard.once("error", reject); guard.once("exit", () => reject(Error("serve-check keeper exited"))); });
  guard.once("exit", () => process.exit(1));
  const child = spawn("npm", ["run", "--silent", ...process.argv.slice(2)], {stdio:["ignore", "inherit", "inherit"]});
  process.on("message", (message: {type:string;command:string;ready:Record<string,unknown>}) => {
    if (message.type !== "then") return;
    const ready = message.ready;
    const command = spawn("sh", ["-c", message.command], {env:{...process.env,
      SNAPBACK4_SERVE_JSON:JSON.stringify(ready),
      ...(typeof ready.backendUrl === "string" ? {SNAPBACK_URL:ready.backendUrl, BACKEND_URL:ready.backendUrl} : {}),
      ...(typeof ready.frontendUrl === "string" ? {FRONTEND_URL:ready.frontendUrl} : {})}, stdio:["ignore","pipe","pipe"]});
    for (const stream of ["stdout", "stderr"] as const) command[stream].on("data", data => process.send?.({type:"then-output", stream, data:data.toString()}));
    command.once("error", () => process.send?.({type:"then-result", code:127}));
    command.once("close", (code, signal) => process.send?.({type:"then-result", code:code ?? (signal === "SIGINT" ? 130 : signal === "SIGTERM" ? 143 : 1)}));
  });
  process.send?.({type:"started", keeper:guard.pid});
  child.once("error", () => process.send?.({type:"exited", code:null}));
  child.once("exit", (code, signal) => process.send?.({type:"exited", code, signal}));
}

 const candidates=await page.evaluate(()=>[...document.querySelectorAll('body *')].filter(e=>e.childElementCount===0&&!['INPUT','SCRIPT','STYLE'].includes(e.tagName)&&getComputedStyle(e).display!=='none'&&getComputedStyle(e).visibility==='visible'&&e.getBoundingClientRect().top>innerHeight).map(e=>(e.textContent??'').trim()).filter(Boolean));
 let sample;for(const text of candidates){const after=renderedText(page,text,{exact:true});if(await after.count()!==1)continue;await page.evaluate(()=>window.scrollTo(0,0));const before=await old.renderedText(page,text,{exact:true}).count();const begin=performance.now();await waitForFresh(after,null,2000);const inView=await after.evaluate(e=>{const r=e.getBoundingClientRect();return r.top>=0&&r.bottom<=innerHeight});sample={beforeCount:before,afterCount:1,inView,ms:Math.round(performance.now()-begin)};break;}
 rows.push({lane:name,candidateCount:candidates.length,...sample,ok:sample?.inView===true});
 });}finally{await web.stop();}
 },{cwd,env,deployment:'deploy',timeoutMs:60000,thenTimeoutMs:90000});}catch{rows.push({lane:name,ok:false,failure:'copied frontend proof refused'});}
 console.log(JSON.stringify(rows.at(-1)));
}
writeFileSync(join(import.meta.dirname,'text-measurements.json'),JSON.stringify({method:'actual copied Expo dist and prod:serve; real password login; unique below-fold leaf; baseline kit0.4.42 vs DX12; count and on-screen geometry only',rows},null,2)+'\n');

} finally { rmSync('/tmp/dx12-replay/text',{recursive:true,force:true}); }
import test from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import {mkdtempSync, mkdirSync, writeFileSync, readFileSync, rmSync, symlinkSync} from 'node:fs';
import {join, resolve} from 'node:path';
import {tmpdir} from 'node:os';
import {execFileSync} from 'node:child_process';
import {pathToFileURL} from 'node:url';
import {serveCheck, withServeScript, startDev, devAccounts, startServe, deploy, cli, command} from '../dist/proof.js';
const root=resolve(import.meta.dirname,'../../..'), bin=join(root,'target/debug/snapback4');
const env={SNAPBACK4_BIN:bin};
const schema=readFileSync(join(root,'crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/staging.md'),'utf8').match(/```quarry\n([\s\S]*?)```/)[1];
function scratch(t){const cwd=mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(),'dx12-'));t.after(()=>rmSync(cwd,{recursive:true,force:true}));return cwd;}
async function project(t){const cwd=scratch(t);mkdirSync(join(cwd,'snapback'));writeFileSync(join(cwd,'snapback/schema.q'),schema);await deploy('production','snapback',{cwd,env});return cwd;}
function script(cwd,body){writeFileSync(join(cwd,'package.json'),JSON.stringify({type:'module',scripts:{'probe:serve':'node serve.mjs'}}));writeFileSync(join(cwd,'serve.mjs'),body);}
const ready='console.log(JSON.stringify({backendUrl:"http://127.0.0.1:1",frontendUrl:"http://127.0.0.1:2",password:"PRIVATE-SERVE-SENTINEL"}));setInterval(()=>{},1000);';
const opts=cwd=>({cwd,env,script:'probe:serve',deployment:'production',timeoutMs:3000,thenTimeoutMs:3000,stopTimeoutMs:100});
test('serve callback receives private readiness, returns its value and releases the lock', {skip:process.platform==='win32'},async t=>{
 const cwd=await project(t);script(cwd,ready);
 const value=await withServeScript('probe:serve',async (ready,signal)=>{assert.equal(signal.aborted,false);assert.equal(ready.password,'PRIVATE-SERVE-SENTINEL');return 42;},opts(cwd));
 assert.equal(value,42);assert.equal((await cli(['serve','production','--wait-stopped'],{cwd,env})).code,0);
});
test('callback failure and hung callback still release serve group and lock', {skip:process.platform==='win32'},async t=>{
 const cwd=await project(t);script(cwd,ready);
 await assert.rejects(withServeScript('probe:serve',()=>{throw Error('business assertion');},opts(cwd)),/business assertion/);
 let signal;
 await assert.rejects(withServeScript('probe:serve',(_,abort)=>{signal=abort;return new Promise(()=>{});},{...opts(cwd),thenTimeoutMs:50}),/E_SERVE_THEN_TIMEOUT/);
 assert.equal(signal.aborted,true);assert.equal((await cli(['serve','production','--wait-stopped'],{cwd,env})).code,0);
});
test('then command gets readiness and nonzero status; descendants and hung commands are reaped', {skip:process.platform==='win32'},async t=>{
 const cwd=await project(t);script(cwd,ready);
 const result=await serveCheck({...opts(cwd),then:`node -e 'const s=JSON.parse(process.env.SNAPBACK4_SERVE_JSON); if(s.password!=="PRIVATE-SERVE-SENTINEL" || process.env.SNAPBACK_URL!==s.backendUrl || process.env.FRONTEND_URL!==s.frontendUrl) process.exit(9); process.exit(7)'`});
 assert.equal(result.commandCode,7);assert.ok(!JSON.stringify(result).includes('PRIVATE-SERVE-SENTINEL'));
 const hung=await serveCheck({...opts(cwd),then:'node -e "setInterval(()=>{},1000)"',thenTimeoutMs:80});
 assert.ok(hung.failures.some(f=>f.code==='E_SERVE_THEN_TIMEOUT'),JSON.stringify(hung));
 assert.equal((await cli(['serve','production','--wait-stopped'],{cwd,env})).code,0);
});
test('startup hang names script and startup deadline without exposing output', {skip:process.platform==='win32'},async t=>{
 const cwd=await project(t);script(cwd,'setInterval(()=>{},1000)');let called=false;
 const report=await serveCheck({...opts(cwd),timeoutMs:100,then:()=>{called=true;}});
 assert.equal(called,false);assert.match(report.failures.find(f=>f.code==='E_SERVE_NO_JSON').message,/probe:serve.*100 ms/);
});
test('dev accounts use real password signup and ordinary operator provisioning, and refuse serve',async t=>{
 const cwd=await project(t), options={roles:{admin:1,member:1},provision:{name:'provision'}};
 const host=await startDev({cwd,env});
 try {
  const sessions=await devAccounts(host,options);assert.equal(sessions.admin[0].kind,'user');
  for(const [role,accounts] of Object.entries(sessions))for(const account of accounts){
   const response=await fetch(host.url+'/q/myRole',{method:'POST',headers:{authorization:`Bearer ${account.token}`,'content-type':'application/json'},body:'{"args":{}}'});
   assert.equal((await response.json()).data.role,role);
  }
  assert.equal((await host.devAccounts({roles:{member:1},provision:{name:'provision'}})).member.length,1);
 }finally{await host.stop();}
 const serve=await startServe('production',{cwd,env});try{await assert.rejects(devAccounts(serve,options),/E_DEV_ACCOUNTS/);}finally{await serve.stop();}
});
test('packed CLI propagates command status and literal withServeScript guide recipe runs', {skip:process.platform==='win32'},async t=>{
 const cwd=await project(t), pack=scratch(t);
 const packed=JSON.parse(execFileSync('npm',['pack','--ignore-scripts','--json','--pack-destination',pack],{cwd:join(root,'packages/snapback4'),encoding:'utf8'}))[0];execFileSync('tar',['-xzf',join(pack,packed.filename),'-C',pack]);
 mkdirSync(join(cwd,'node_modules'));symlinkSync(join(pack,'package'),join(cwd,'node_modules/snapback4'));
 const module=pathToFileURL(join(pack,'package/dist/proof.js')).href;
 script(cwd,`import {startServe,serveUntilSignal} from ${JSON.stringify(module)}; const host=await startServe('production'); console.log(JSON.stringify({backendUrl:host.url})); await serveUntilSignal([host]);`);
 const result=await command(process.execPath,[join(pack,'package/bin/snapback4.js'),'serve-check','--deployment','production','--script','probe:serve','--then','node -e "process.exit(7)"'],{cwd,env,secret:true});
 assert.equal(result.code,7,result.stderr);assert.equal(JSON.parse(result.stdout).commandCode,7);
 const guide=readFileSync(join(root,'crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/testing.md'),'utf8');
 const block=guide.split('<!-- DX12 RUN SERVE BEGIN -->')[1].match(/```js\n([\s\S]*?)```/)[1];writeFileSync(join(cwd,'run.mjs'),block);
 const ran=await command(process.execPath,['run.mjs'],{cwd,env,secret:true});assert.equal(ran.code,0,ran.stderr);
 const installed=await import(module);assert.equal(typeof installed.withServeScript,'function');assert.equal(typeof installed.devAccounts,'function');
});

test('serve shutdown failure cannot earn success from a successful command', {skip:process.platform==='win32'},async t=>{
 const cwd=await project(t);script(cwd,'process.on("SIGTERM",()=>process.exit(7));'+ready);
 const preload=join(cwd,'npm-term.cjs');writeFileSync(preload,'if((process.argv[1]?.endsWith("npm-cli.js") || process.argv[1]?.endsWith("/npm"))) process.on("SIGTERM",()=>{});');
 const report=await serveCheck({...opts(cwd),env:{...env,NODE_OPTIONS:`--require=${preload}`},then:'true',stopTimeoutMs:3000});
 assert.equal(report.commandCode,0);assert.equal(report.ok,false,JSON.stringify(report));assert.ok(report.failures.some(f=>f.code==='E_SERVE_STOP_FAILED'),JSON.stringify(report));
});

const locator=kit.renderedText(page,'Late proof').filter({visible:true}).and(page.locator('p')).first();
if(process.argv[2]==='count')assert.equal(await locator.count(),1);

// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#15-wave-26-proof-ergonomics-dx9 — refuse duplicate scopes and ambiguous evidence; use real input events.
test('DX9 proof scopes, substring ambiguity and typed HTML inputs', {timeout:30000}, async t => browser(t, async page => {
  await page.setContent('<main><p>Saved order 42</p><p>Saved order 43</p><input type="date"><input type="datetime-local"><input type="file"><input type="text"></main>');
  const within=page.locator('main');
  await assert.rejects(kit.waitForFresh(kit.renderedText(page,'Saved'),null,500),/E_PROOF_AMBIGUOUS.*2 rendered matches.*within.*exact/);
  assert.equal(await kit.waitForFresh(kit.renderedText(page,'order 42'),null,500),'Saved order 42');
  const scoped=kit.renderedText(page,'order 42',{within}).filter({visible:true});
  await assert.rejects(kit.waitForFresh(scoped,null,{within,timeoutMs:500}),/E_PROOF_USAGE.*within.*twice/);
  await page.evaluate(()=>{window.events=[];document.querySelectorAll('input').forEach(el=>el.addEventListener('input',()=>window.events.push(el.type)));});
  await kit.fillRN(page.locator('[type=date]'),'2026-10-24');
  await kit.fillRN(page.locator('[type=datetime-local]'),'2026-10-24T12:34');
  await kit.fillRN(page.locator('[type=file]'),{name:'evidence.txt',mimeType:'text/plain',buffer:Buffer.from('uploaded evidence')});
  await kit.fillRN(page.locator('[type=text]'),'ordinary RN input');
  assert.equal(await page.locator('[type=date]').inputValue(),'2026-10-24');
  assert.equal(await page.locator('[type=datetime-local]').inputValue(),'2026-10-24T12:34');
  assert.equal(await page.locator('[type=file]').evaluate(el=>el.files[0].name),'evidence.txt');
  assert.equal(await page.locator('[type=text]').inputValue(),'ordinary RN input');
  assert.ok((await page.evaluate(()=>window.events)).includes('date'));
  assert.ok((await page.evaluate(()=>window.events)).includes('datetime-local'));
}));

test('DX12 Expo web body overflow uses document scrolling and never hidden text', {timeout:30000}, async t => browser(t, async page => {
  await page.setViewportSize({width:700,height:300});
  await page.setContent('<style>html{height:100%}body{margin:0;overflow:hidden;height:100%}#root{min-height:100vh}</style><div id="root"><div style="height:1800px"></div><p>Below fold receipt</p><p hidden>Hidden receipt</p></div>');
  const locator=kit.renderedText(page,'Below fold receipt');
  assert.equal(await kit.waitForFresh(locator,null,2000),'Below fold receipt');
  assert.ok(await locator.evaluate(el=>{const box=el.getBoundingClientRect();return box.top>=0&&box.bottom<=innerHeight;}));
  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page,'Hidden receipt').count(),0);
}));
test('DX12 beginForm constrains a subset while preserving trusted click and value matching', {timeout:30000}, async t => browser(t, async page => {
  await page.route('http://dx12.test/**',route=>route.fulfill({body:'<html></html>',contentType:'text/html'}));
  await page.goto('http://dx12.test/?witness=web');
  await page.setContent(`<button>Save</button><script type="module">${witnessSource.replace(/export /g,'')}
  const app={ready:Promise.resolve({ok:true}),mutate:async()=>({state:'sent',id:'real'}),client:{viewer:()=> 'user:one',onWrite:()=>()=>{},mutate:async()=>({state:'sent',id:'real'})}};
  registerWebWitness(app,{save:{kind:'mutation',name:'save'}});
  document.querySelector('button').onclick=()=>app.mutate({kind:'mutation',name:'save'},{id:'handler-id',title:'expected',extra:null});</script>`);
  await page.waitForFunction(()=>window.__snapback4Witness);
  const button=page.getByRole('button');
  await button.evaluate(el=>window.__snapback4Witness.beginForm('save',{title:'expected'},el));await button.click();
  assert.equal((await page.evaluate(()=>window.__snapback4Witness.formResult())).state,'sent');
  await button.evaluate(el=>window.__snapback4Witness.beginForm('save',{title:'wrong'},el));await button.click();
  const wrong=await page.evaluate(()=>window.__snapback4Witness.formResult());assert.equal(wrong.why.code,'E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED');
}));

test('DX12 non-propagated body overflow still clips evidence', {timeout:30000}, async t => browser(t, async page => {
  await page.setContent('<style>html{overflow:auto}body{height:30px;overflow:clip;margin:0}</style><div style="height:90px"></div><p>Clipped body receipt</p>');
  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page,'Clipped body receipt').count(),0);
}));

test('DX12 propagated body clip cannot invent viewport scrolling', {timeout:30000}, async t => browser(t, async page => {
  await page.setViewportSize({width:700,height:300});
  await page.setContent('<!doctype html><style>body{overflow:clip;margin:0}</style><div style="height:900px"></div><p>Outside clipped viewport</p>');
  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page,'Outside clipped viewport').count(),0);
}));

codex
The shutdown code matches the narrowed contract: it signals npm and its shells together, checks the live group and store lock before forced cleanup, and fails on unsuccessful npm status it receives. I’m checking the replay cleanup paths and viewport clipping regression next; I won’t run service-backed tests.

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4/tests/dx12_output.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/tests/dx12_output.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..92ced0246f
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/tests/dx12_output.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,25 @@
+//! DX12 changes presentation only; full evidence and refusal status survive.
+use std::{fs,path::PathBuf,process::{Command,Output}};
+struct App(PathBuf);
+impl App {
+ fn new()->Self {let p=std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("dx12-output-{}-{}",std::process::id(),{static N:std::sync::atomic::AtomicUsize=std::sync::atomic::AtomicUsize::new(0);N.fetch_add(1,std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed)}));fs::create_dir_all(p.join("snapback")).unwrap();Self(p)}
+ fn write(&self,text:&str){fs::write(self.0.join("snapback/schema.q"),text).unwrap();}
+ fn run(&self,args:&[&str])->Output{Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_snapback4")).args(args).current_dir(&self.0).output().unwrap()}
+ fn ok(&self,args:&[&str])->String{let r=self.run(args);assert!(r.status.success(),"{args:?}: {r:?}");String::from_utf8(r.stdout).unwrap()}
+}
+impl Drop for App{fn drop(&mut self){let _=fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);}}
+const SOURCE:&str="table notes:\n  title: text <=80\n  public 'fixture'\nquery list():\n  return notes first 20 by .id\n";
+#[test]fn deploy_rollback_and_releases_lead_with_verdict_and_keep_live_identity(){
+ let app=App::new();app.write(SOURCE);
+ let first=app.ok(&["deploy","production"]);assert!(first.starts_with("deploy verdict: READY"),"{first}");assert!(first.lines().nth(1).unwrap().starts_with("live:"),"{first}");assert!(!first.contains("decision "),"{first}");
+ app.write(&SOURCE.replace("<=80","<=160"));let second=app.ok(&["deploy","production"]);assert!(!second.contains("preview:"),"{second}");
+ let rolled=app.ok(&["rollback","production","--to","1"]);assert!(rolled.starts_with("rollback verdict: READY"),"{rolled}");assert!(rolled.lines().nth(1).unwrap().starts_with("live:"),"{rolled}");
+ let short=app.ok(&["releases","production"]);assert!(short.starts_with("releases verdict: READY"),"{short}");assert!(short.lines().count()<=4,"{short}");
+ let json:serde_json::Value=serde_json::from_str(&app.ok(&["releases","production","--json"])).unwrap();assert!(json["live"].is_object());assert!(json["events"].is_array());
+ let review=app.run(&["deploy","other","--review"]);assert!(review.status.success(),"{review:?}");let full=format!("{}{}",String::from_utf8_lossy(&review.stdout),String::from_utf8_lossy(&review.stderr));assert!(full.contains("decision ")||full.contains("REVIEW"),"{full}");
+}
+#[test]fn gates_remain_explicit_and_blocking_with_next_steps(){
+ let app=App::new();app.write("table docs:\n  photo: image <=10000 ?\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- deny\n  online only\ninvariant docs always .photo != null\nmutation add(photo: image <=10000 ?):\n  return insert docs {photo}\n");
+ let r=app.run(&["deploy","production"]);assert!(!r.status.success());let err=String::from_utf8_lossy(&r.stderr);assert!(err.contains("deploy verdict: REFUSED")&&err.contains("N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE")&&err.contains("Intended:")&&err.contains("Repair:"),"{err}");
+ let json:serde_json::Value=serde_json::from_slice(&app.run(&["deploy","production","--json"]).stdout).unwrap();assert_eq!(json["ok"],false);assert!(json["security"]["securityReviews"].as_array().unwrap().iter().any(|d|d["gated"]==true&&d["acknowledged"]==false));
+}

 let binary;

       ? "E_STAGING: account seeding requires serve on a fresh deploy --staging with password identity; production and dev refuse before creating users"

+/** Fresh password accounts with ordinary operator provisioning, for startDev acceptance proofs.

@@ -250,3 +252,13 @@ A staging clock offset belongs to its deployment store. Every CLI advance and
 redeploy prints the retained offset; rerunning against that directory keeps
 advanced business time. Use a fresh directory for an independent run, and sample

+
+## Development acceptance accounts
+
+For password accounts under `startDev`, use `devAccounts(dev, plan)` or
+`dev.devAccounts(plan)`. It accepts the same `roles`, `provision`, `service`,
+`args(account)` and `timeoutMs` plan as `provisionAccounts`, and returns the same
+role-keyed arrays with email, password, principal and token. It signs up real
+password users and runs the ordinary operator mutation; rules still decide.
+It refuses staging/production hosts before creating users. Use persistent
+`provisionAccounts` for production, and `seedAccounts` for fresh staging batches.

+The same liveness check is available from Node:
 
 ```js
 import { serveCheck } from "snapback4/proof";
@@ -508,6 +509,31 @@ console.log(JSON.stringify(result));

 ```
 
+For any test against a running app, use `npx snapback4 serve-check --deployment production --script prod:serve --then 'npm run client:calls'`.
+Never write a serve driver: the runner starts the real npm script, waits for its
+first JSON line, runs the command, stops the entire group and waits for the lock.
+The command receives `SNAPBACK4_SERVE_JSON` (the complete readiness object),
+`SNAPBACK_URL`/`BACKEND_URL` and `FRONTEND_URL`. Treat readiness as credentials:
+never print it. The exit status is the command's status; a startup or shutdown
+failure makes an otherwise successful command fail. `--then-timeout-ms` defaults
+to 120000; a hung command is reported separately from missing startup JSON,
+early serve exit, children ignoring TERM and a retained store lock.
+
+For an in-process proof callback, use this complete example (set `probe:serve`
+and its deployment first). Callback work must honor its AbortSignal or bound
+its own browser/network operations; cleanup runs even when the callback throws.
+<!-- DX12 RUN SERVE BEGIN -->
+```js
+import assert from "node:assert/strict";
+import { withServeScript } from "snapback4/proof";
+await withServeScript("probe:serve", async (ready, signal) => {
+  const response = await fetch(`${ready.backendUrl}/auth/config`, { signal });
+  assert.equal(response.ok, true);
+  assert.equal((await response.json()).development, false);
+}, { deployment: "production", thenTimeoutMs: 30000 });
+```
+<!-- DX12 RUN SERVE END -->
+

import { after as teardownAfter } from "./helpers/teardown.mjs";
// H9 controls can load the unchanged 0.4.29 proof/witness modules via KIT2_BASELINE_DIST.
import test from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { mkdtempSync, rmSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync, mkdirSync, readdirSync, statSync } from 'node:fs';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join, resolve } from 'node:path';
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
import { createRequire } from 'node:module';
import { chromiumExecutable } from '../../../witness/lib.mjs';
const dist = process.env.KIT2_BASELINE_DIST ?? resolve(import.meta.dirname, '../dist');
const kit = await import(pathToFileURL(join(dist, 'proof.js')));
const witnessSource = readFileSync(join(dist, 'witness-web.js'), 'utf8');
async function browser(t, run) {
  const cwd = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'kit2-contract-'));
  teardownAfter(t, () => rmSync(cwd, {recursive:true,force:true}));
  await kit.withBrowser({cwd, executablePath:process.env.CHROME ?? chromiumExecutable()}, async context => run(await context.newPage(), cwd));
}
test('DX9 renderedText defaults to substring matching for a row with more than the needle', {timeout:30000}, async t => browser(t, async page => {
  await page.setContent('<section aria-label="Rows"><p>Proof needle — more row text</p><input value="Proof needle"></section>');
  const within = page.getByRole('region', {name:'Rows'});
  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page, 'Proof needle — more row text', null).count(), 1, 'null options mean the defaults');
  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page, 'Proof needle', {within, exact:true}).count(), 0, 'exact remains opt-in');
  await assert.rejects(kit.waitForFresh(kit.renderedText(page, 'Proof needle', {within, exact:true}), null, 50), /a substring match exists; pass a RegExp or `exact: false`/);
  const row = kit.renderedText(page, 'Proof needle', {within, exact:false});
  assert.equal(await row.count(), 1, 'explicit substring matches the whole rendered row');
  assert.equal(await kit.waitForFresh(row, null, {timeoutMs:500}), 'Proof needle — more row text');
  assert.equal(await kit.waitForFresh(kit.renderedText(page, /Proof needle/, {within}), null, 500), 'Proof needle — more row text');
  await assert.rejects(kit.waitForFresh(row, 'Proof needle — more row text', {timeoutMs:1000}), /locator.*last count=1.*Proof needle.*present but unchanged/);
  await assert.rejects(kit.waitForFresh(kit.renderedText(page,'missing'), null, {timeoutMs:1000}), /last count=0.*text=null.*absent/);
}));
test('DX8 renderedText finds a full status line anywhere in a scrollable RN-style root', {timeout:30000}, async t => browser(t, async page => {
  await page.setViewportSize({width:700,height:400});
  await page.setContent(`<main style="height:90px;overflow-y:auto"><div style="height:600px"></div><div><span>Pump </span><span>logged.</span></div></main>`);
  const root=page.locator('main');
  assert.equal(await kit.renderedText(page,'Pump logged.',{within:root,exact:false}).count(),1);
  assert.equal(await kit.waitForFresh(kit.renderedText(page,'Pump logged.',{within:root,exact:false}),null,500),'Pump logged.');
  assert.ok(await root.evaluate(element=>element.scrollTop)>0,'proof observation scrolls the match into view');
}));

test('DX8 exhaustPages follows opaque cursors and refuses incomplete traversal', async () => {
  const cursors=[];
  const rows=await kit.exhaustPages(async cursor=>{
    cursors.push(cursor);
    if(cursor===null)return {data:[{id:'newest'}],next:'opaque:page-2',complete:true,cut:null};
    return {data:[{id:'older'}],next:null,complete:true,cut:null};
  });
  assert.deepEqual(cursors,[null,'opaque:page-2']);
  assert.deepEqual(rows,[{id:'newest'},{id:'older'}]);
  await assert.rejects(kit.exhaustPages(async()=>({denied:{code:'PRIVATE',message:'no'}})),/E_PROOF_PAGE.*PRIVATE/);
  await assert.rejects(kit.exhaustPages(async()=>({loading:true})),/E_PROOF_PAGE.*loading/);
  await assert.rejects(kit.exhaustPages(async()=>({data:[],next:null,cut:null})),/E_PROOF_PAGE.*complete must be true/);
  await assert.rejects(kit.exhaustPages(async()=>({data:[],next:'would-continue',complete:false,cut:'meter'})),/E_PROOF_PAGE.*incomplete.*meter/);
  await assert.rejects(kit.exhaustPages(async()=>({data:[],next:null,complete:true,cut:null,existence:'partial'})),/E_PROOF_PAGE.*partial existence/);
  await assert.rejects(kit.exhaustPages(async()=>({data:[],next:'same',complete:true,cut:null}),{maxPages:2,label:'board'}),/E_PROOF_PAGE.*board.*repeated cursor/);
});

+DX12 shutdown-observation boundary: this deliberately uses the harness's group
+SIGTERM, including npm and its shell. Nonzero npm exits that the driver receives
+are failures. A SIGTERM exit plus an empty group and released lock proves those
+process/ownership observations; it does not prove that every application's
+shutdown hook completed successfully. npm or an intermediate shell may terminate
+before collecting a descendant's exit status. A synthetic handler installed in
+npm tests the observable-exit branch only; it is not shipped or required. No
+claim about every descendant's exit code is made. Applications needing external
+cleanup guarantees must assert those effects in their proof command/callback.

// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#16-wave-27-process-checks-dx10 — keep group identity pinned until inspection, then reap it on EOF.
import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
import { constants } from "node:os";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
process.stdin.resume();
process.on("SIGTERM", () => {}); process.on("SIGINT", () => {});
if (process.argv[2] === "--keeper") {
  process.stdin.once("end", () => process.kill(0, "SIGKILL"));
  process.stdin.once("error", () => process.kill(0, "SIGKILL"));
  process.send?.("ready");
} else {
  const guard = spawn(process.execPath, [fileURLToPath(import.meta.url), "--keeper"], {stdio:["pipe", "ignore", "inherit", "ipc"]});
  guard.stdin!.on("error", () => {});
  process.stdin.once("end", () => guard.stdin!.destroy());
  process.stdin.once("error", () => guard.stdin!.destroy());
  await new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => { guard.once("message", () => resolve()); guard.once("error", reject); guard.once("exit", () => reject(Error("serve-check keeper exited"))); });
  guard.once("exit", () => process.exit(1));
  const child = spawn("npm", ["run", "--silent", ...process.argv.slice(2)], {stdio:["ignore", "inherit", "inherit"]});
  process.on("message", (message: {type:string;command:string;ready:Record<string,unknown>}) => {
    if (message.type !== "then") return;
    const ready = message.ready;
    const command = spawn("sh", ["-c", message.command], {env:{...process.env,
      SNAPBACK4_SERVE_JSON:JSON.stringify(ready),
      ...(typeof ready.backendUrl === "string" ? {SNAPBACK_URL:ready.backendUrl, BACKEND_URL:ready.backendUrl} : {}),
      ...(typeof ready.frontendUrl === "string" ? {FRONTEND_URL:ready.frontendUrl} : {})}, stdio:["ignore","pipe","pipe"]});
    for (const stream of ["stdout", "stderr"] as const) command[stream].on("data", data => process.send?.({type:"then-output", stream, data:data.toString()}));
    command.once("error", () => process.send?.({type:"then-result", code:127}));
    command.once("close", (code, signal) => process.send?.({type:"then-result", code:code ?? (signal ? 128 + (constants.signals[signal] ?? 0) : 1)}));
  });
  process.send?.({type:"started", keeper:guard.pid});
  child.once("error", () => process.send?.({type:"exited", code:null}));
  child.once("exit", (code, signal) => process.send?.({type:"exited", code, signal}));
}
